AVC (3017.TW): Liquid cooling rising penetration and AI servers ramp-up 
to drive growth ahead; Buy
19 October 2026 | 3:24AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue 
sequential growth through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +42% YoY/ 
+15% QoQ, driven by: (1) new AI server racks ramp-up, driving the overall liquid 
cooling penetration rate. As we highlighted in Oct (report link), we expect AI server 
rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 63k/ 110k/ 164k 
in 2026-28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks. (2) Its major ASIC AI 
server customer’s new model ramp-up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate. (3) Its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26. And (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit 
the lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s 
net income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain 
Buy.

### Page 2

Earnings revisions: We factor in AVC’s Sep revenue and revise down our 2026E net 
income by -1%, mainly on lower revenues in 4Q26E, reflecting the slow consumer 
electronics under high memory costs and AI servers model transition. We revise up our 
2027-28E net income by 4% / 0%, mainly on higher revenues and GMs. Our 2027-28E 
revenue is revised up by 1% / 1%, mainly on a higher contribution from AI server liquid 
cooling components. 2027-28E GM are revised up to reflect the rising liquid cooling 
components driving profitability.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price and 
update our target P/E multiple to 30.6x 2027E EPS (vs. 30.3x previously), which is still 
derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. Our target P/E 
multiple is above AVC’s historical average +1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on AVC riding on the rising penetration rate of liquid-cooled AI servers. 
With our updated target P/E and earnings estimates, we raise our 12-month target price 
to NT$4,736 (from NT$4,513). Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,736 on a target P/E multiple of 30.6x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部仍看好時碩未來營運，主要是考量液冷散熱零件業務隨著客
戶訂單逐步放量及出貨規模擴大 ，帶動 2H26 業績大幅提升。 此外 ，
航太事業成長前景仍佳，工業事業亦可望持續增長，明年展望依舊
正向。研究部預估時碩 2026 年 EPS 3.52 元(+162%YoY)，2027 年
EPS 4.63 元(+32%YoY)。以明年獲利為評價基礎，給予 20 倍本益
比，目標價 93 元。

重點摘要：

1. 受惠AI趨勢，液冷散熱訂單將帶動3Q26業績強勁增長： 
時碩3Q26合併營收上升至18.18億元(+24%QoQ，+43%YoY)，主
要是受惠AI資料中心液冷相關零組件的貢獻增加，令7、8、9
月單月營收持續創新高 。考量營收擴大， 規模效應顯現， 預期
時碩3Q26毛利率將提升至 24.5%，營益率提升至 8.8%，預估
3Q26稅後淨利1.38億元(+101%QoQ，+34%YoY)，預估3Q26EPS 
1.26元。

2. 液冷散熱產品進入爬坡期，4Q26營運淡季不淡： 
展望4Q26， 雖然年底通常為時碩汽車、工業業務的淡季， 但受
惠散熱零件仍處在出貨爬坡期，預期季度營收與獲利仍有望
再墊高。研究部預估時碩 4Q26合併營收18.85億元(+4%QoQ，
+55%YoY)，4Q26稅後淨利1.40億元(+1%QoQ，+113%YoY)， 預估
4Q26EPS 1.27元 。 全年度來看 ， 研究部預估時碩2026年合併營
收將提升至65.25億元(+31%YoY)，年度毛利率24.1%，營益率
7.1%，稅後淨利3.87億元(+161%YoY)，預估2026年EPS 3.52
元。

3. 航太業務維持成長動能，積極拓展新商機 
受惠於全球航太產業需求穩健及上游關鍵零組件產能吃緊，
時碩管理層持續看好航太業務前景 ，其中天陽航太營運成長
率預估可達15%～20%。 此外 ， 公司積極擴充泰國航太產能 ， 規
劃建置鋁合金及不鏽鋼生產線，以掌握全球航太供應鏈重組
及非紅供應鏈商機 ， 中長期目標為貢獻約10億元營收 。 除既有
航太業務外，時碩亦積極拓展人形機器人及半導體設備等新
興應用領域。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

在人形機器人方面， 時碩已投入諧波減速器及關節零組件開
發，並與國際及中國大陸主要廠商展開合作。半導體設備方
面，時碩持續開發相關精密零組件，未來將配合泰國新廠布
局 ， 爭取台灣客戶海外工廠的供應商機 。 整體而言 ， 隨著航太
業務穩健成長，以及人形機器人與半導體設備等新領域逐步
拓展 ， 將有助於公司建立更多元的營運成長動能 ， 降低對傳統
汽車產業的依賴。

4. 明年業績續升，預估2027年EPS 4.63元： 
展望2027年 ， 考量日系大廠的液冷散熱訂單將延續至明年 ， 而
且還有美系散熱大廠客戶訂單與磁浮壓縮機關鍵訂單等待發
酵 ， 加上航太業務仍具偏強增速 ， 預期時碩2027年業績將持續
提升。研究部預估時碩2027年合併營收74.20億元(+14%YoY)，
年度毛利率 25.0%， 預估2027年EPS將進一步提升至 4.63元。

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

2026년 10월 20일

ESG 
SK하이닉스 솔리다임 상장, 성장과 주주가치를 잇는 조건

▶Analyst 박세연 shannon@hanwha.com 3772-7406

[주요 데이터 및 차트]

NPS 낸드 사업 반기 실적  
→ 순이익 0.13조원에서 5.84조원으로

주: NPS는 솔리다임의 모회사  
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

공모 구조별 SK하이닉스 몫 
→ 거래 직후 가치 같음(가정) 
구분 신주 100% 구주매출 100% 
SK하이닉스 몫 약 129조원 약 116조원 + 현금 13.3조원 
현금이 남는 곳 솔리다임 NPS → SK하이닉스 
전액 환원 가정 
주당 
공모 현금은 
솔리다임에 
약 1만 8,300원 
(종가의 1.3%)

주: 공모 전 지분가치 1,000억달러·조달 100억달러 가정 
자료: 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

솔리다임 상장 이 SK 하이닉스 주주에게 이익 이 될지 는 상장 여부가 아
니라 조 건이 정 합니 다. 보도 된 기업가치 1,000 억달 러(블 룸버그)는 약 
136조 원으 로 SK하이 닉 스 시 가총 액 의 13% 규모 입 니다. 확 인할 조건은 
세 가지, 상장 전 출자 가격 과 공모 자 금의 쓰임, 환원 계획 입니 다. 신
주라면 미국 투자 의 수익 을, 구주 매 출 이 라면 매각 대금 이 주주 에 게 돌
아오는 길을 봐야 합 니다. 판단 시점은 금 융위 원 회 유권 해 석과 10 월 
28 일 실적 발 표, 12월 3 일 재공 시 입 니 다.

다롄 생산기지, 미국 공장 검토 
미국은 다롄 공장(옛 인텔 다롄)을 장비 반입 포괄 허가 명단에서 빼고 
연간 허가로 전환(BIS, 2025). SK하이닉스 매출의 68.8%는 미국 판매
법인에서 발생. 솔리다임은 미국 동부에 낸드 공장 검토(Reuters, 
2026.9). 세계 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2030년 2.3배(2024년 대비)로 
늘 전망이라 대용량 SSD 수요가 커지는 것도 배경(IEA, 2025)

해외 상장에도 중복상장 규율, 이사회 의무가 관건 
금감원장은 금융위·금감원 모두 중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답변
(10.19 국정감사). 국내 증권신고서 대상 여부는 금융위 유권해석 대
기. 해외 상장이라 거래소 특례심사는 받지 않고 주주동의는 권고 사
항. SK㈜·SK이노베이션·SK텔레콤은 NPS 신주 인수에 총 11.1억달러를 
약정. 사업 경쟁력과 자금 조달의 필요성, 기존 주주가치에 미치는 영
향을 함께 살펴볼 필요[그림7·12]

자금의 쓰임이 관건, 환원 가정 시 주당 약 1만8,300원 
공모가가 공정하다는 가정 아래, 거래 직후 SK하이닉스의 지분가치와 
현금 합계는 신주·구주 모두 약 129조원으로 같고 현금이 남는 곳만 
다름. 구주매출 100억달러 중 NPS를 거쳐 SK하이닉스에 귀속되는 현
금을 전액 배당하면 주당 약 1만8,300원. 10월 13일 종가의 1.3%, 
2025 사업연도 주당 배당의 약 6배(회사의 배당 계획이 아닌 가정 계
산). 신주 발행으로 조달한 자금을 투자하면 주주가치는 투자수익에 달
림[표4].

2 
 I. 시장이 묻는 세 가지

1. 세 가지 질문과 확인 결과

솔리다임 상장, 
시장의 세 가지 질문 
 솔리다임 상장 보도(Bloomberg, 10/7) 뒤 시선은 SK하이닉스 주주가치로 향했다. 시장
의 질문은 세 가지다. 왜 미국 상장인가(II·III장), 상장 전 지분은 누구에게 가는가(IV
장), 주주에게 돌아올 몫은 얼마인가(V장). 
당국은 중복상장 
규율 적용 입장 
금감원장 · 10.19 국정감사 
 금감원장은 10월 19일 국정감사에서 금융위원회와 금융감독원 모두 솔리다임 상장을 
중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답했다. 다만 해외 상장이라 거래소 특례심사는 받지 않
고, 주주동의가 필수인 물적분할 자회사도 아니다. 표4 가정(추가 프리IPO 제외)대로면 
SK하이닉스의 솔리다임 실질 지분은 약 85~86%다(6월 말 95.08%). 국내 증권신고서 
대상인지는 금융위원회 유권해석을 기다리고 있다. 
미국 공시 체계, 
모회사는 이미 편입 
 미국 상장으로 엄격한 공시 규율을 따르면 기업가치를 높게 평가받을 수 있다는 것이 
자기구속(bonding) 가설이다(Coffee, 2002). 미국에 교차상장한 외국 기업의 토빈 q는 
같은 나라의 미국 미상장 기업보다 16.5% 높았다는 연구도 있다(Doidge 외, 2004, 
1997년 표본).  
Coffee, J. C., “Racing T owards the T op?: The Impact of Cross-Listings and Stock Market Competition on International Corporate 
Governance”, Columbia Law Review 102(7)

SK하이닉스는 7월 10일 나스닥 ADR 상장으로 이미 미국 공시 체계에 편입됐다. 솔리
다임 상장의 추가 효과는 자금 조달과 투자자 접근성 측면에서 따로 평가해야 한다. 
삼성전자와 차이 
1.2%p 이내 
 보도일과 다음 거래일 이틀 동안 SK하이닉스는 프리IPO 보도 때 5.2%, 주관사 선정 
때 6.8% 내렸다. 삼성전자(4.0%, 6.7%)보다 각각 1.2%p, 0.1%p 더 내렸다. 두 구간 모
두 SK하이닉스는 삼성전자와 비슷하게 움직였다(한 종목과 견준 이틀 관찰)[그림1]. 
10월 13일 낙폭은 
솔리다임 공시 없던 날 
 10월 13일 코스피는 5.46% 내린 6,035.35에 마감했고, SK하이닉스는 10.09%, 삼성전
자는 7.39% 내렸다. 이날 솔리다임 관련 공시는 없었다. SK하이닉스는 삼성전자보다 
2.7%p 더 내렸지만, 이 비교로는 그 원인을 가려낼 수 없다. 
[그림1] 솔리다임 보도 전후 이틀 주가 → 두 보도 구간 모두 삼성전자와 차이는 1.2%p 이내

3

2. 연구가 본 중복상장 할인의 조건

연구가 본 할인 요인, 
가치 이전 위험 
선행 연구 
 중복상장 할인의 근거로는 흔히 지배주주와 일반주주 사이의 이해상충이 꼽힌다. 대표
적인 예가 지배주주를 위해 회사의 자산과 이익을 옮기는 터널링이다(Johnson 외, 
2000). 지배권이 현금흐름권을 웃돌수록 기업가치는 낮았다(Claessens 외, 2002). 한국 
기업집단을 다룬 연구도 계열사 간 인수와 사모 발행이 소수주주 몫에 영향을 준다는 
점을 보여 줬다(Bae·Kang·Kim, 2002; Baek·Kang·Lee, 2006)[표1]. 그래서 시장은 계열
사 거래의 가격 산정 근거를 눈여겨본다. 
자금의 쓰임 따라 
모회사 반응도 달라 
 자회사 상장이 늘 악재는 아니다. 미국에서는 자회사 공모 발표 때 모회사 주가가 오르
는 경향이 있었다(Schipper·Smith, 1986). 공모 자금을 주로 부채 상환·배당에 쓰겠다고 
밝힌 표본의 누적초과수익률은 +6.63%, 투자 목적으로 보유하겠다고 밝힌 표본은 
−0.01%였다(Allen·McConnell, 1998). 할인 여부는 상장 자체보다 공모 구조와 자금의 
쓰임에 달려 있다. 
같은 상장도 
분위기 따라 달리 읽혀 
 같은 중복상장도 시장 분위기에 따라 다르게 읽힌다. 미국 기술주 거품기에는 자회사 
주식이 비싸게 거래돼, 모회사 시가총액이 보유 자회사 지분가치보다 낮아지는 가격 오
류까지 나타났다. 공매도가 어려워 이 오류는 한동안 풀리지 않았다(Lamont·Thaler, 
2003). 심리가 높았던 시기 뒤에는 가치를 매기기 어려운 주식의 수익률이 상대적으로 
낮았다(Baker·Wurgler, 2006). 솔리다임 보도일과 다음 거래일 이틀 동안 SK하이닉스 
주가는 8월과 10월 두 구간 모두 삼성전자와 비슷하게 움직였다[그림1].

[표1] 중복상장 관련 주요 연구 → 가치 이전 위험이 할인 요인으로 꼽히고, 자금을 회사 밖에 쓰겠다고 밝힌 표본의 반응이 더 좋음 
연구 핵심 발견 인용 수 
Johnson 외(2000, AER) 지배주주를 위해 회사의 자산과 이익을 옮기는 「터널링」 개념 정리 2,211 
Claessens 외(2002, JF) 동아시아 기업에서 지배권이 현금흐름권을 웃돌수록 기업가치 하락 3,842 
Bae·Kang·Kim(2002, JF) 한국 기업집단 계열사 인수에서 인수회사 주가는 하락, 지배주주는 다른 계열사 가치 상승으로 이득 1,170 
Baek·Kang·Lee(2006, JF) 한국 재벌 계열사의 사모 증권 발행에서 발행가를 통해 지배주주 쪽으로 가치 이전 576 
Schipper·Smith(1986, JFE) 자회사 공모 발표 때 모회사 주가 상승(같은 회사의 유상증자 발표와 반대) 362 
Allen·McConnell(1998, JF) 공모 자금을 주로 부채 상환·배당에 쓰겠다고 밝힌 표본 누적초과수익률 +6.63%, 투자 목적 보유 표본 −0.01% 174 
Lamont·Thaler(2003, JPE) 미국 기술주 자회사 상장에서 모회사 시가총액이 보유 자회사 지분가치보다 낮은 가격 오류가 공매도 제약으로 지속 660 
Doidge 외(2004, JFE) 미국에 교차상장한 외국 기업의 토빈 q 가 같은 나라 기업보다 16.5% 높음(1997년 표본) 1,627 
Baker·Wurgler(2006, JF) 투자 심리가 높았던 시기 뒤에는 가치평가·차익거래가 어려운 주식의 수익률이 상대적으로 낮음 6,011

주: 인용 수는 OpenAlex 기준(2026년 10월 14일 조회)

4 
 II. 지정학: 다롄 공장과 미국 거점

1. 미·중 승인 아래 놓인 다롄, 미국 거점을 검토하는 솔리다임

낸드 생산 거점, 
중국 다롄 
인수 대금 88억달러 
 솔리다임 낸드는 인텔에서 넘겨받은 중국 랴오닝성 다롄 공장에서 만든다. SK하이닉스
는 인텔 낸드 사업 인수에 2021년 12월 66억달러, 2025년 3월 22억달러를 치렀다(SK
하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026). 다롄은 192단 제품을 만든다(Blocks & Files, 2026.9.18). 
장비 반입은 
해마다 허가 
미국 상무부 · 2025.8.29 
 미국 상무부는 2025년 8월 29일 장비 반입 포괄 허가(VEU) 명단에서 다롄 공장(옛 
Intel Semiconductor (Dalian))과 SK하이닉스 중국 법인을 빼기로 했다. 고시의 시행일
은 2025년 12월 31일이다(BIS, 2025)[그림4]. 대신 해마다 필요한 미국산 장비 계획을 
내고 승인받는 방식으로 바꿨고, 회사는 2025년 12월 2026년분 연간 허가를 받았다
(SK하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026)[그림2]. 생산은 이어지지만, 장비 반입은 해마다 미국의 허
가를 받아야 한다. 
중국 쪽 조건도 
12월 이후 해제 신청 
 중국 시장감독관리총국(SAMR)은 인텔 낸드 인수를 승인하며 2021년 12월부터 5년간 
기업용 SSD(PCIe·SATA)의 중국 판매가 상한, 생산 확대, 경쟁사 진입 지원 등을 조건
으로 붙였다. 판매가는 거래 조건이 같으면 승인 전 24개월 평균을 넘지 못한다
(SAMR, 2021). 회사는 이 조건 때문에 2026년 중국에 파는 낸드 제품 값을 크게 올리
기 어렵다고 밝혔다. 5년이 지나면 해제를 신청할 수 있고, 승인 여부는 당국이 정한다
(SK하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026)[그림2]. 다롄은 미국의 장비 반입 허가와 중국의 가격·생산 
조건, 두 가지 틀 안에서 운영된다.

[그림2] SK하이닉스 F-1/A 원문 → 장비 반입은 해마다 허가, 중국 판매가는 5 년 조건, 2025년 매출의 68.8%는 미국 판매법인

5 
 2. 미국 공장 검토와 남은 일정

미국 공장 검토, 
공모 자금이 재원 후보 
 솔리다임은 미국 동부(뉴욕주가 후보로 거론)에 낸드 공장을 짓는 방안을 검토하고 있
다(Reuters, 2026.9). 미국 공장에는 다롄에 들이기 어려운 최신 장비를 들일 수 있다. 
신주로 조달한 공모 자금은 그 건설 재원이 될 수 있다. 솔리다임 낸드 공장과는 별개
로, SK하이닉스는 미국 인디애나에 첨단 패키징 공장을 짓고 있다. 2028년 10월 클린룸
을 열고 2029년 하반기 양산할 계획이다(착공식 발표, 2026.8.27). 이 사업은 요건을 채
우면 미국 정부 보조금 최대 4.58억달러와 대출 최대 5.7억달러를 받을 수 있다(SK하이
닉스 F-1/A, 2026). 미·중 승인과 상장 일정은 [그림3]에 모았다.

[그림3] 솔리다임과 다롄 공장을 둘러싼 일정 → 다롄은 미·중 두 조건 아래, 미국 공장은 생산 거점 분산·상장은 자금 조달의 선택지

주: VEU 제외는 BIS(2025), 인수 대금·중국 조건·연간 허가는 SK하이닉스 F-1/A(2026), 나머지는 DART 공시와 언론 보도 기준. 당사 투자의견과 무관 
자료: SEC, DART, Bloomberg, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림4] 미국 연방관보 원문: VEU 명단 제외 → 다롄 공장(옛 인텔 다롄)이 이름으로 명시, 2025년 12월 31일 시행

6 
 III. 에너지 전환: 전력이 고르는 저장장치

1. 데이터센터 전력 제약과 대용량 SSD

전력이 모자라면 
용량당 전력이 관건 
 국제에너지기구(IEA)는 세계 데이터센터 전력 사용이 2024년 415TWh에서 2030년 
945TWh로 늘 것으로 본다[그림5]. 2024년 사용량의 45%가 미국이다. 전력이 제약이 
되면 같은 전력에 더 많이 담는 저장장치가 유리하다. 솔리다임은 122TB QLC(셀당 4
비트 낸드) SSD로 저장장치를 꾸리면 HDD·SSD 혼용 구성보다 설치 공간은 최대 4분
의 1, 저장장치 전력은 최대 84% 준다고 밝혔다(Solidigm, 2024). 
미국 공장은 공급망, 
상장은 자금 조달 
 NPS의 낸드 사업 반기 매출은 1년 새 3.7배가 됐다[그림9]. SK하이닉스 전체 매출의 
68.8%가 미국 판매법인에서 나왔다(2025년; SK하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026)[그림2]. 데이터
센터 전력 증가도 미국이 이끌었다[그림6]. 미국 공장은 공급망을, 미국 상장은 자금 조
달을 넓히는 수단이 될 수 있다.

[그림5] 세계 데이터센터 전력 사용 → 2030년 945TWh로 2024년의 2.3배, 2024년 사용량의 45%가 미국

자료: IEA, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림6] IEA 원문: 지역별 데이터센터 전력 사용 → 2017년 이후 증가는 미국이 주도

자료: IEA, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

7 
 IV. 지배구조: 상장 전 지분

1. 지분 사슬과 계열사 출자

계열사의 
NPS 출자 약정 
약정 합계 11.1억달러 
 솔리다임은 SK하이닉스의 미국 법인 NPS(SK hynix NAND Product Solutions)가 지분 
96.86%를 가진 회사다(6월 말). 올해 SK㈜(2.5억달러), SK이노베이션(미국 법인 경유 
3.8억달러), SK텔레콤(4.8억달러, 4년 분할)이 NPS 신주를 받기로 약정했다[표2]. NPS는 
외국법인이어서 국내 계열회사 주식 소유 제한(공정거래법 제18조)의 대상이 아니다. 
직접 지분의 가치는 
SK㈜ 몫 
 SK㈜는 SK스퀘어 지분 32.2%를, SK스퀘어는 SK하이닉스 지분 20.0%를, SK하이닉스
는 NPS 지분 98.16%를 갖고 있어 지분율을 곱한 SK㈜의 NPS 간접 지분은 약 6.3%
다. 여기에 직접 지분 1.27%가 더해졌다(6월 말). SK㈜는 별도·연결 재무제표 모두에서 
NPS를 관계기업으로 처리한다. 상장에 따른 시장가치 변화가 장부에 바로 잡히지는 않
지만, 직접 지분의 가치 변동은 지분을 가진 SK㈜에 귀속된다[그림7].

[그림7] SK그룹 지분도와 솔리다임 상장 사슬 → 지주회사·자회사·손자회사가 모두 상장된 사슬 아래, 계열사가 NPS에 직접 출자

주: 지분율은 2026년 6월 말 기준(SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분은 7월 ADR 신주 상장 뒤 주식 수 기준) 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표2] 공시 원문 발췌: 계열사의 NPS 출자 → 세 회사 약정 11.1억달러, SK텔레콤은 642주를 3,971억원에 취득하기로 결정 
공시 원문 발췌 
SK텔레콤(2026.6.29, 7.23 정정공시) 발행회사 SK hynix NAND Product Solutions Corp.(미국). 취득주식수 642주, 취득금액 397,129,047,957원, 취득 후 지분 0.62%. 현금출자, 취득예정일 2026-07-31(7.23 정정) 
같은 공시: 취득목적 「당사 AI 사업과의 시너지 확보 측면에서, SK hynix NAND Product Solutions Corp.의 지분 확보 추진」 
SK㈜(2026.8.14) 「총 약정금액은 USD 250,000천이며, 당반기말 현재 USD 133,891천의 출자가 완료되었습니다」. 같은 주석의 SK텔레콤 약정: 「총 약정금액은 USD 480,000천이며, … 2026년 6월 25일부터 4 년간」 
SK이노베이션(2026.8.14) 「2026년 7 월 23일자로 관계기업인 SK hynix NAND Product Solutions Corp.에 USD 143.4백만을 추가 출자하였습니다」. 약정 총액 USD 380,000천(SK Innovation America 경유)

자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

8 
 2. 출자 단가와 실적

기준이 다른 
세 가치 지표 
 SK㈜ 반기보고서의 NPS 장부가와 지분율로 역산한 장부가 기준 NPS 가치는 약 16조
원이다. SK텔레콤의 취득 단가(642주, 3,971억원)에 신주 발행 전 주식 수(101,875주)를 
곱하면 약 63조원이다. 장부가 역산과 거래 단가는 산출 기준이 달라, 두 값의 차이만으
로 출자 가격의 적정성을 판단할 수는 없다. 낸드 사업 반기 순이익은 지난해 상반기 
0.13조원에서 올해 상반기 5.84조원으로 늘었다[그림9]. 보도된 상장 기업가치(약 136
조원)는 NPS가 아니라 솔리다임 기준 값이다[그림8]. 
출자 단가는 
계열사 공시로 확인 
 출자 단가는 신주를 받은 계열사의 공시와 주석에서 확인할 수 있다[표2]. SK텔레콤 약
정 잔여분(4년 분할)과 외부 프리IPO가 어떤 단가와 규모로 이뤄지는지가 상장 전 SK
하이닉스 주주의 몫을 정할 것이다.

[그림8] 기준이 다른 세 가치 지표(NPS·솔리다임) → 평가 대상·산출 방식이 달라 직접 비교에는 한계

주: 5~6월은 SK㈜ 별도 장부가(1,997억원) ÷ 지분율(1.27%)로 역산값. 세 값은 산출 기준이 달라 직접 비교에 한계. 
자료: DART, 블룸버그, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림9] NPS 낸드 사업 반기 실적 → 매출 3.7배, 순이익 0.13조원에서 5.84조원으로

주: SK하이닉스 반기보고서 연결 주석의 NPS 요약재무 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

9 
 3. 솔리다임의 비중

기업가치로는 
시가총액의 약 13% 
10월 13일 종가 기준 
 NPS 낸드 사업이 SK하이닉스 연결에서 차지하는 몫은 상반기 매출의 9.3%, 순이익의 
4.3%, 자산의 5.7%, 순자산의 3.1%다. 순이익 비중이 낮은 것은 연결 순이익에 금융상
품 평가이익 등이 반영됐기 때문이다. 보도된 기업가치(약 136조원)는 10월 13일 SK하
이닉스 시가총액(1,054조원)의 약 13%다(회계 비중과는 기준이 다름)[그림10]. 
회사 입장은 
확정된 사항 없음

SK하이닉스는 8월 5일과 9월 4일 보도 관련 공시에서 솔리다임이 경쟁력 강화를 위해 
다양한 방안을 검토하고 있으나 확정된 사항은 없다고 밝혔다. 다음 재공시 예정일은 
12월 3일이다. SK텔레콤의 NPS 취득 공시와 SK하이닉스의 보도 관련 공시 원문은 [그
림11]에 실었다.

[그림10] SK하이닉스 안의 NPS(낸드) 비중 → 회계 숫자로는 3~9%, 보도된 기업가치로는 시가총액의 약 13%

주: NPS 낸드 사업 ÷ SK하이닉스 연결(2026년 6월 말). 연결 순이익에는 금융상품 평가이익 등 금융수익(상반기 82.9조원)이 반영. 기업가치 136조원은 1,000억달러 × 1,360원(SBS 
Biz, 2026.10.8 환산) 
자료: DART, KRX, 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림11] 공시 원문: SK텔레콤의 NPS 취득과 SK하이닉스의 보도 관련 공시 → 642주 3,971억원 취득 결정, 상장은 「확정된 사항 없음」

자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

10 
 4. 해외 상장에 적용되는 규율

8월부터 
엄격한 특례심사 
거래소 규정 · 8월 3일 
 금융위원회는 7월 31일 거래소 상장·공시 규정 개정을 의결해 8월 3일부터 중복상장을 
엄격한 특례심사 대상으로 바꿨다. 일반주주 권익을 고려하지 않는 비대칭적 중복상장
은 엄격히 금지한다는 취지다. 모회사 이사회는 독립 특별위원회 심의를 거쳐 주주영향
평가, 주주보호 방안, 주주 소통·동의 확인, 찬반 결의, 공시라는 다섯 가지 의무를 진다. 
금융위원회는 이 의무를 자회사 해외상장 때에도 적용한다고 밝혔다(금융위, 
2026.7.31)[그림12]. 
거래소 심사 미적용, 
당국은 규율 적용 입장 
 해외 상장은 거래소의 중복상장 특례심사를 거치지 않는다. 다만 금감원장은 금융위·금
감원 모두 중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답했다(10.19 국정감사). 주주동의가 필수인 
곳은 물적분할 자회사뿐이고, 솔리다임 같은 일반 자회사는 권고 대상이다(금융위, 
2026.7.31). 국회에는 자회사 상장 때 모회사 주총 특별결의를 받게 하는 자본시장법 개
정안(안도걸 의원 등 14인, 9월 9일 발의)이 정무위원회에 있다[표3].

[그림12] 금융위원회 중복상장 제도개선 구조도(원문) → 모회사 이사회 5 대 의무는 자회사 해외상장 때에도 적용

자료: 금융위원회, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표3] 솔리다임 상장에 적용되는 규율 → 거래소 심사는 미적용이나 당국은 규율 적용 입장, 이사회 의무가 핵심 
규율 내용 솔리다임 적용 
공정거래법 제18조 지주회사는 자회사 외, 자회사는 손자회사 외 국내 계열회사 주식 소유 금지. 손자회사는 국내 계열회사 주식을 100% 가질 때만 소유 대상 아님(외국법인) 
거래소 중복상장 특례심사 자회사의 독립성, 이사회 5 대 의무 이행과 찬성 결의, 주주동의 여부 심사 미적용(미국 상장) 
모회사 이사회 5 대 의무 주주영향평가, 주주보호 방안, 주주 소통·동의 확인, 특별위원회 심의 뒤 찬반 결의, 단계별 공시 적용(금융위원회) 
주주동의 물적분할 자회사는 필수, 그 밖의 자회사는 권고. 3% 초과 의결권 제한, 출석 과반·전체 4분의 1 이상 권고(일반 자회사) 
자본시장법 개정안 자회사 상장 때 모회사 주총 특별결의(의안 2221240, 9월 9일 발의; SBS Biz, 2026.10.8) 정무위원회 계류

주: 중복상장 규정은 2026.7.31 의결, 8.3 시행. 해외 상장 적용은 금융위원회 보도참고자료(2026.7.31) 기준 
자료: 국가법령정보센터, 금융위원회, 국회 의안정보시스템, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 11

ESG [한화리서치]

11 
 V. 주주가치: 공모 구조와 자금의 쓰임

1. 공모 구조별 SK 하이닉스 주주 손익

거래 직후 기준, 
신주든 구주든 가치 중립 
 공모가가 공정하면 신주 발행이든 구주매출이든 SK하이닉스 몫(지분가치와 현금의 합)
은 같다. 차이는 현금이 어디에 남느냐다. 신주를 내면 현금은 솔리다임에 남아 미국 공
장 등 투자 재원이 될 수 있고, 구주를 팔면 대금은 먼저 NPS에 들어오고, NPS의 배당
을 거쳐야 SK하이닉스의 환원 재원이 될 수 있다[표4]. 
전액 환원 가정 시 
주당 약 1만8,300원 
가정 · 100억달러 
 보도된 규모인 100억달러(약 13.6조원)를 구주매출로 조달하고, NPS가 이를 배당해 SK
하이닉스가 받은 현금(NPS 지분 98.16%분)을 다시 전액 특별배당한다고 가정하면 주
당 약 1만8,300원이다. 10월 13일 종가의 1.3%, 2025 사업연도 주당 배당(3,000원)의 6
배다. 다만 8월에 결정한 소각용 자사주 매입(40조원)과 견주면 3분의 1 규모다. 회사의 
배당 계획이 아니라 규모를 가늠하기 위한 가정 계산이다. 
추가 출자의 
가격과 조건 
 공모 전 외부 지분이 어떤 값과 조건으로 정해지는지도 SK하이닉스 주주의 몫을 좌우
한다. 출자 이후의 가치 상승분은 그 지분을 가진 계열사에 돌아간다. 다만 과거 출자 
단가가 상장 때의 기업가치보다 낮았다는 것만으로 기존 주주에게 불리한 거래였다고 
볼 수는 없다. 남은 출자와 프리IPO의 단가가 공정가에 가까울수록 SK하이닉스 주주의 
몫은 지켜진다. 
공모 구조의 평가, 
쓰임과 투자수익이 결정 
실적 개선 뒤의 값 
 보도된 기업가치(약 136조원)는 최근 실적 개선 뒤에 나온 값이다[그림8]. 이 값이 적정
한지는 이익이 이어지는지에 달려 있다. 구주매출은 판 지분만큼 지금의 평가를 현금으
로 바꾸는 길이고, 신주 발행은 지분을 일부 내주는 대신 미국 투자 재원을 솔리다임에 
넣어 수익으로 돌려받는 길이다. 신주로 조달한 자금을 미국 공장에 쓴다면, 주주가치는 
투자수익과 지정학적 위험을 줄이는 효과에 달려 있다. 어떤 공시가 나오면 무엇을 볼
지는 [표7]에 정리했다.

[표4] 공모 구조별 SK하이닉스 몫(가정) → 공정가면 지분가치와 현금의 합은 같고, 현금이 남는 곳만 다름 
구분 신주 발행 100% 구주매출 100% 
조달액(가정) 100억달러(약 13.6조원) 같음 
현금이 남는 곳 솔리다임(미국 공장 재원 가능) NPS·SK하이닉스(환원 재원 가능) 
SK하이닉스 실질 지분(6월 말 95.08%) 약 86.4% 약 85.3% 
SK하이닉스 몫(보도 기업가치 기준) 약 129조원 약 116조원 + 현금 13.3조원 
전액 환원 가정 시 주당 공모 현금은 솔리다임에 약 1만 8,300원(10/13 종가의 1.3%)

주: 1,000억달러를 공모 전 지분가치로 보고 100억달러를 조달한다고 둔 가정 계산. 거래 직후의 산술 비교로 주가 영향·투자수익·세금은 반영하지 않음. 당사 투자의견과 무관 
자료: DART, 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 12

ESG [한화리서치]

12 
 2. 종목별 영향과 판단 변수

종목마다 
받는 영향이 달라 
 솔리다임 상장이 SK그룹 상장사에 미치는 영향은 종목마다 다르다. SK하이닉스의 솔리
다임 실질 지분은 6월 말 공시 기준 95.08%로, 공모 구조·출자 가격·자금의 쓰임이 주
요 변수다. SK스퀘어는 SK하이닉스 지분을 통해 간접적으로 영향을 받는다. SK하이닉
스의 환원 정책과 보유 자산가치가 함께 반영된다. SK㈜·SK텔레콤·SK이노베이션은 
NPS 직접 지분의 가치 변화와 남은 출자 단가를 함께 봐야 한다[표5]. 
상장은 낸드 사업의 
가격표 
 솔리다임 상장은 SK하이닉스 연결 안에 있던 낸드 사업에 시장 가격을 따로 붙인다. 시
가총액의 약 13%로 보도된 기업가치가 독립된 값으로 평가받는 계기다[그림10]. 회사
는 8월에 40조원 규모의 소각용 자사주 매입을 결정해 환원 원칙을 이미 보여 줬다. 시
장이 확인하려는 것은 구주매출로 현금이 생길 때 이 원칙이 이어지는지다. 변동성이 
큰 시기에는 주주보호 방안을 먼저 공개할수록 시장의 신뢰를 빨리 얻을 수 있다. 
판단을 바꿀 
변수 네 가지 
 이 판단을 바꿀 변수는 네 가지다. 상장 시점은 메모리 업황과 미국 기술주 공모 시장의 
분위기에 달려 있다. 미국의 2027년분 연간 허가는 다롄 장비 반입을 정한다. 중국 당
국의 조건 해제는 중국 판매가와 생산 의무를 바꾼다. 미국 공장은 투자 규모와 보조금 
조건이 확정돼야 수익성을 따질 수 있다. 
해외 상장, 
다른 그룹의 선례 
 해외 상장에는 거래소 심사가 적용되지 않지만, 당국은 중복상장 규율을 적용한다는 입
장이다. 금융위원회의 유권해석은 해외 자회사 상장 전반의 선례가 된다. 현대차(2024
년 10월)와 LG전자(2025년 10월)는 인도 법인을 현지에 상장하며 보유 주식을 파는 
구주매출을 택했다. 국회 법안이 대통령령으로 해외 증시까지 넓히면 모회사 주총 특별
결의가 해외 상장에도 걸린다(SBS Biz, 2026.10.8). 해외 자회사 상장을 검토하는 그룹은 
이 법안의 진행을 함께 봐야 한다.

[표5] SK그룹 상장사별 솔리다임 노출과 판단 조건 → 같은 상장이라도 종목마다 봐야 할 조건이 다름 
종목 지분 보유 경로 영향 경로 주요 판단 조건 
SK하이닉스 NPS 98.16% × 솔리다임 96.86%(실질 95.08%) 신주면 지분 희석·현금은 솔리다임에, 구주매출이면 NPS에 현금 유입. 주가는 메모리 업황이 주변수 공모가의 적정성, 신주·구주 비율, 조달 자금의 투자수익과 환원, 남은 출자 단가 
SK스퀘어 SK하이닉스 지분 20.0%를 거친 간접 노출 보유 자산가치와 SK하이닉스 환원 정책이 함께 반영 SK하이닉스의 환원 정책, 순자산가치 대비 할인율  
SK㈜ NPS 직접 1.27%(6월 말), SK스퀘어·SK하이닉스를 통한 간접 보유 직접 지분의 경제적 가치가 SK㈜에 귀속 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향, 총약정 2.5억달러 중 남은 납입액 
SK텔레콤 NPS 0.62%(642주, 취득 결정 기준) 직접 지분의 가치 변화, 4년 분할 출자 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향, 총약정 4.8억달러 중 남은 납입액 
SK이노베이션 미국 법인 경유 NPS 출자(약정 3.8억달러) 미국 법인이 보유한 NPS 지분의 가치 변화와 추가 출자 부담 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향,  잔여 약정의 납입 부담과 출자 재원

주: 지분은 2026년 6월 말 공시 기준(SK스퀘어는 7월 ADR 뒤, SK텔레콤은 취득 결정 기준). 노출 경로 정리로 투자의견·순위 아님 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 13

ESG [한화리서치]

13 
 3. 확인할 공시와 결론

상장 사슬이 
네 단계로 
 SK㈜·SK스퀘어·SK하이닉스는 이미 모두 상장돼 있다. 솔리다임까지 상장하면 SK㈜에
서 솔리다임으로 이어지는 수직 사슬에 상장사가 네 곳 놓인다. 상장사가 늘어날수록 
각 단계의 주주에게 가치와 현금이 어떻게 전달되는지를 설명하는 일이 중요해진다. 
보호 방안의 
내용과 이행 
 가이드라인은 주주보호 방안의 예로 자회사 주식 현물배당과 자사주 소각을 든다. SK하
이닉스 이사회가 주주영향평가와 보호 방안을 언제, 어떤 내용으로 공시하는지가 확인
할 첫 문서다. 미국 공장의 필요성과 자회사 상장의 필요성은 따로 따질 문제다. 이사회
가 설명할 것은 다른 조달 수단과 견줘 상장이 기존 주주에게 유리한지, 그 근거는 무엇
인가다. 다음 재공시 예정일(12월 3일)을 포함해 확인할 일정은 [표6]에 정리했다. 
결론: 상장 기대보다 
조건 확인이 먼저

SK하이닉스 주가의 큰 방향은 여전히 메모리 업황이 정한다. 솔리다임 상장은 SK하이
닉스 안의 낸드 사업이 어떻게 평가받는지를 정하는 변수다. 그 평가를 가르는 조건은 
세 가지다. 상장 전 출자 단가가 적정한지, 공모 자금이 미국 투자와 모회사 가운데 어
디로 가는지, 투자수익이나 환원 계획이 구체적인지다. SK㈜·SK텔레콤·SK이노베이션은 
NPS 지분의 가치와 남은 출자 부담을 함께 봐야 한다. 상장과 미국 공장은 아직 확정
되지 않았다. 지금은 상장 기대를 미리 더하기보다, 금융위원회의 유권해석과 이사회의 
주주보호 방안, 12월 3일 재공시에서 이 조건을 확인할 때다.

[표6] 확인할 공시와 일정 → 공모가보다 상장 전 단가와 공모 자금의 쓰임이 먼저 
시점 확인할 것 
10월 28일 SK하이닉스 3 분기 실적발표(컨퍼런스콜, 10월 14일 안내공시): 솔리다임·NPS 관련 질의 여부 
11월 19일 SK하이닉스 소각 목적 자사주 매입(40조원, 8월 19일 결정)의 매입 기간 종료: 실제 취득 규모와 소각 이행 
12월 3 일 SK하이닉스 솔리다임 관련 보도 관련 공시의 재공시 예정일(9월 4 일 공시) 
12월 중국 인수 조건 5 년 경과: 해제 신청 여부와 당국 결정. 미국 2027년분 장비 반입 허가 여부(2026년분은 지난해 12월 발급) 
수시 SK하이닉스 이사회 특별위원회 구성, 주주영향평가, 주주보호 방안(자회사 주식 현물배당·자사주 소각 등) 공시 
수시 NPS 추가 신주 발행 단가: SK텔레콤 약정 잔여분(4년 분할), 외부 프리IPO 투자(보도) 
미정 금융위원회 유권해석: 솔리다임 상장의 국내 증권신고서 제출 대상 여부 
미정 솔리다임 미국 공장 부지·투자 규모 발표 
2027년(Bloomberg) 솔리다임 뉴욕 상장: 신주와 구주매출의 비율, 공모 자금의 쓰임, 상장 뒤 SK하이닉스 지분율

주: 예정 일정은 바뀔 수 있음 
자료: DART, 국회 의안정보시스템, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표7] 공시별 판단 기준 → 상장 여부보다 어떤 공시가 나오느냐가 판단을 바꿈 
공시로 확인되는 것 주주가치 판단의 초점 함께 볼 증거 
신주 중심 공모 지분율 하락보다 조달 자금의 투자수익 투자 규모·집행 일정·보조금 조건 
구주매출, 대금이 NPS에 남음 매각 가격과 현금의 재투자 계획 자금 사용 계획·세금·비용 
구주매출, 모회사 환원 구체화 SK하이닉스로 오는 현금과 환원 규모 배당·소각 결의와 집행 일정 
계열사 추가 출자·프리IPO 거래 당시의 가격·규모와 조건 외부 투자자 가격·주식 권리 
상장 지연·철회 기존 낸드 사업가치의 지속성 실적·현금흐름·대체 자금 조달

주: 판단 기준의 정리로 전망·투자의견 아님 
자료: 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026년 10월 20일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 당사는 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현
재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 당사는 이 자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박세연) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 이 자료는 특정 종목의 매수·매도를 권유하지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터
가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 
책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices
card-acceptance costs that had previously been surcharged
– and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-
plus’ approach to pricing. To the extent they can pass on
these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was

strong enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal
that demand is still outstripping supply.
• This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages
growth has been moderate for some years, though the
above-average increase in award wages this year will add
a little. Not all firms will seek to pass these cost increases
on, but some will, including because they have limited
scope to absorb the cost increase into margins.
• A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it

limits the imagination away from finding alternative,
efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with
recent academic research suggesting that firms form
expectations and set prices according to what is
happening in their own industry and experience, while
putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central
bank credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many
consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when
supply shocks abound.

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused
considerable upset this month, even though it is a reversion
to historical and international practice. The card schemes
used to impose “no-surcharge” rules, until the RBA banned
these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The
turnaround represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its
longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people to
choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer

made sense in a largely cashless economy. In any case, least-
cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in
the hands of the merchants who actually bear the cost.
In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker
price, leaving the total cost to the consumer unchanged, just
more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers
have absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first
place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated that around

16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though,
some firms have lifted their prices by more than any reasonable
estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway
coffee has been the centre of consumer discontent.
Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main
instigator of this month’s slump in consumer sentiment. Petrol
‘Cost-Plus’ costs
Forecast changes

• A full update to our forecasts was published in our October
Market Outlook  on Thursday 15 October.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and
the before-and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week
shows that higher interest rates also contributed to the decline.
In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the
RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses passing on increased

costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to
make that increase stick – is exactly the kind of inflationary
environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been
subjected to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from
pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel to
water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which
for decades were a reliable source of cost reductions, are
nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins

intact, demonstrates that demand is not weak enough to
prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations
might be higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this
as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair
Work Commission did hand down an above-average increase in
award wages this year and this was probably passed through
to prices in hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base

wages growth has been moderate for some years. Business
surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue
lately. This is why we were puzzled by the passage in the RBA
minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported
in business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate
consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU) has risen. It is
a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to
overshoot your forecasts, as the latest labour market data have

done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.
Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do
not have the market power, though coffee may be an exception
because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And
as noted above, large firms with everyday-low-price strategies
will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply chain.
Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on.
Some observers might see this as corporate greed, but another

way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality.
“Entrenched expectations that cost increases
can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against,
but only indirectly."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
It turns out that cost-plus pricing is especially common in
areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated
a lot in recent years.
There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The

failures of so many building firms a few years ago, squeezed
between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and
fixed-price contracts, were a case in point. Normally, there is
a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect
firms never to pass cost increases through to prices.
The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits
the imagination away from finding responses that might save

costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost
efficiency. It was equally limiting in times of soft demand: in
the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a belief
that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they
could not possibly accept cost increases, including in wages.
Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be
consistent with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle
of a central bank governor complaining that wages growth

was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to
economise on it and thereby boost productivity?
A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of
supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing as the ‘inflation
expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation,
but it is related. The stronger the tendency towards cost-plus
pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the

distinction matters. The recent academic literature includes
some fascinating empirical evidence  from New Zealand that
suggests that beliefs about current and future overall inflation
have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are
shaped less by economy-wide inflation developments than by
what is happening to their costs and in their industry.
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot

like firms (in aggregate) setting prices according to expected
inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something
quite different is going on. If firms’ beliefs about future
economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing
decisions, then neither does the credibility of a central bank’s
commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism to
get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand
enough to convince firms that cost-plus will not fly in the

market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers
or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when supply shocks
abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade
or even self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that
happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.
The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can
be reduced. That is more likely to be a question for regulators
than for central banks.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
bias towards an October or December decision. Williams
stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in
this cycle given known information. And Waller also showed
confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are

doing some of the FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest
rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating that
one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation
near the 2.0%yr medium-term target. However, as discussed
in this month’s Market Outlook , capacity pressures are likely
to see inflation consistently above target, rather than below,
in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity in the
housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to

remain modestly restrictive over the forecast horizon and will
also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade
account. At USD133.5bn, the September result was a whisker
inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More
impressively, throughout 2025 and 2026 year-to-date the
surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the
average of 2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth
of export growth is as impressive as the headline annual gain of

36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese
investment in neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time,
both trade and income receipts from offshore investment should
buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound
foreign investment should moderate the pace of gains against
other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the
evolution of producer prices across Asia, and the likelihood

of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent
decades continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was
one of the key drivers of declining goods prices. While
still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing
development to instead be driven by capital, technology and
energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking
production are also increasingly prioritising resilience, security
and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore more

probably future supply-chain developments will help contain
rising costs through productivity than drive sustained price
declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the
downside, –39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force
participation from August’s near-historic high largely offset.
The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August

was revised up.
Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless,
the take home point from the update is that the labour
market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs
below working-age population growth. We expect the
unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter
financial conditions and cost-of-living pressures restrict
activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast
peak is near at 4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the

communication challenge for the RBA. The September minutes
were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing
on materialising upside risks to inflation. But, with the
unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption
of 4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the
labour market is becoming harder to defend. Still, with a clear
focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our
near-term CPI forecasts in the October Market Outlook  – we

still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in
September, taking the annual rate up to 3.7%yr. Energy prices
were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation
was a touch above consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr).
Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months
(0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4% (3.1%yr) on a

0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport
services and a 0.3% increase in medical care services (2.7%yr
and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release
of the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures,
with the headline and core measures up 6.3%yr and 5.0%yr
respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these
developments, firms across multiple districts citing tariffs,
insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost

pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect
further price increases as contracts/hedges roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is
enough signal in these reports to warrant a follow-up hike
at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee
members believe they are near the end of the tightening cycle
and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman
both showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear

Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Downward revisions to 2027 outlook
This week we have updated our New Zealand economic
outlook for the next year, in particular reflecting the latest
developments in the energy market and key financial
variables (long-term interest rates and the exchange rate).

After last week’s marking down of our forecast for through-
year GDP growth in 2026 to 2.5%yr, this week we have
reduced our forecast of growth in 2027 by 0.3ppts to 2.7%yr.
This downward revision reflects a less positive assessment
of the outlooks for consumption and investment (including
residential investment) considering the more elevated path
assumed for energy prices and a higher near-term track for
long-term interest rates (as discussed in our updated Market

Outlook, published earlier this week).
A partial offset to weaker growth in domestic demand is
provided by a slightly more positive outlook for exports
(particularly exports of manufactured goods and tourism
inflows) because of a downward revision to our expectations
for the NZ dollar. We now expect NZD/USD to end this
year around current levels at 0.56 – 3c lower than expected
previously – before appreciating to around 0.64 over the
course of 2027 as interest rate differentials with the US

gradually close and as the USD appreciates. We think that the
low value of the NZ dollar – in trade-weighted terms now at a
17-year low – helps to explain the recent strong performance
in the previously moribund manufacturing sector, as well as
tourist inflows from key markets such as Australia.
Last week we raised our forecast for CPI inflation at year end
to 4.3%yr, largely on account of recent developments in global
energy prices (although the weak NZ dollar has compounded the

effect). This week we have slightly revised up our forecast for
inflation for the first half of 2027. But because energy prices are
assumed to decline in 2027 from what is now a higher starting
level, our 2027 year-end forecast for inflation has nudged
down 0.1ppts to 1.6%yr. Needless to say, forecasts of headline
inflation, in particular, are subject to significant revision given the
uncertain outlook for the Middle East conflict and uncertainty

about how that will be reflected in global energy prices.
Ahead of next week’s very important Q3 CPI report – which
could prove decisive – we have made no changes to our
forecast for the RBNZ’s policy settings. So, for now we
continue to think that despite the higher near-term inflation
outlook the RBNZ will stick with the strategy communicated
in the September Monetary Policy Statement, i.e. that at the
vv28 October meeting it will pause to assess the impact of this

year’s 50bps of policy tightening, before considering a further
upward adjustment in December.
Turning to this week’s data, the news from the housing market
remained decidedly downbeat. House sales fell a further 4% in
September – the seventh consecutive month of decline – to be
12% lower than a year earlier. With the stock of listings at an
11-year high, the median selling time remains elevated. And
so unsurprisingly, house prices fell for a fourth consecutive

month and are now down 1.6%yr. Prices remain especially
soft in the North Island, with prices down more than 3%yr in
Auckland and more than 4%yr in Wellington. Given upward
pressures on mortgage rates, broader strength in the economy
– including a stronger labour market – will likely be required to
reverse this trend. On that score, MBIE’s measure of online job
advertisements was little changed in September, continuing
the flat trend since the beginning of this year.

In other news, retail spending, as measured by electronic card
payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September following an
unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This was a weaker result than
we had expected based on the recent sawtooth pattern and
our own internal data. We should treat these data with some
suspicion given that for quite a while now the Stats NZ series
seems not to have reflected the spending seen through banks’
own card spending networks, perhaps suggesting changes in the

payments arena may be leaving the official sector data behind.
Even so, unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding late in
the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However,
core spending, which excludes spending on fuel and vehicles,
fell 0.7%m/m. While spending on consumables (groceries)
increased modestly in September, this was at the expense of
spending on ‘nice to haves’. Spending on hospitality suffered a
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
further 2.3%mth fall following a 1.9%mth decline in August,
and spending on apparel replicated the 2.0%mth decline
reported in August. Spending on durable goods also fell
slightly in September (-0.4%mth).
Despite the weak outcome for the August and September

months, retail spending over the September quarter still
eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased
0.4%. Given higher prices, at face value this points to a weak
quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more
light on pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth
noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in the
official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could
point to somewhat stronger growth. Purchases of big-ticket

items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture
and appliances – are underrepresented in the electronic card
spending survey.
Given subdued domestic demand, local hospitality businesses
have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

Document 291: Bank Sector Outlook 2027_ High supply meets a changing financial landscape

Source PDF: all report pdf/Bank Sector Outlook 2027_ High supply meets a changing financial landscape.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 38
Status: ok

### Page 1

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

28 October 2026

www.ing.com/THINK

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 
October 2026

THINK Economic and Financial Analysis

High supply meets a changing financial 
landscape

### Page 2

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Summary 
European banks face another year of high bond supply in 2027, alongside growing 
sustainable issuance and a shifting financial landscape shaped by AT1s, stablecoins 
and regulatory reform 
• Bank bond supply. We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down 
slightly from the €455bn forecast for 2026. Redemptions remain the main driver of 
the high supply, but the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected to push 
supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance reaching €185bn, senior 
unsecured bond supply at €210bn and another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds. 
• Sustainable bank bond supply set for further growth in 2027. Banks issued a 
higher-than-expected amount of sustainable debt in 2026 despite Europe’s ESG 
deregulation efforts. We expect sustainable bank bond supply to continue rising, 
reaching €100bn in 2027, up 7.5% from 2026. Green issuance will remain dominant 
at €85bn, with 20% issued under the EU Green Bond Standard (EuGBS). Sustainable 
loan origination, higher redemptions, revisions to the EU Taxonomy’s technical 
screening criteria and resilient investor demand will continue to support ESG 
issuance. 
• Bank capital – Why AT1 still matters in 2027. AT1 bank bonds will remain an 
important part of European banks’ capital stacks in 2027, with meaningful reform 
still on the back burner. Bonds being called at their first call date remains our base 
case, despite extension risk having risen recently. We expect EUR AT1 supply to rise 
to €20bn, with EUR Tier 2 increasing to €35bn, largely driven by refinancing, while net 
supply is relatively modest. Valuations have improved but still remain at the tighter 
end, while risks are becoming more visible. The yield should continue to support 
demand. Lower reset levels, renewed sovereign-bank concerns, and a softening of 
bank fundamentals could bring more issuer and structure dispersion next year 
• M&A. European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and 2027 should 
stay busy. Domestic deals still dominate, while cross-border consolidation is harder 
but is also picking up as the Unicredit-Commerzbank transaction has shown. 
Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and execution still matter. Expect 
more deals, but a transformation of Europe's banking sector is still some way off. 
• Stablecoins. Stablecoins' market capitalisation grew significantly over the past two 
years, and with this has come widespread scrutiny. Upon broad adoption, the crypto 
asset could have multiple impacts, including on European banks. This could imply a 
shift in their funding base, negative impact on credit provisions, as well as spillover 
risks. However, the sector could also benefit from the technology, notably by issuing 
its own tokens. Several regulatory changes expected in 2027 could alter the impact 
of stablecoins on banks. Despite the multiple risks identified, one should not expect 
banks to surrender ground to fintech issuers without a fight. 
• Solvency II reforms: limited support for bank bonds, while securitisation gains 
some ground. The Solvency II amendments will apply from 30 January 2027, making 
the reforms a key regulatory theme for 2027. A central aim is to free up capital and 
promote long-term investments by European insurers, supporting Europe’s Savings 
and Investment Union. This could also give insurers greater scope to invest in bonds. 
However, we don’t expect any material reallocation towards bank bonds. 
Securitisations should benefit most directly from a more favourable capital 
treatment, while covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should benefit mainly 
indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers.

### Page 3

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

High redemption volumes are expected to keep EUR bank bond supply elevated in 2027

Variables driving next year's bank bond supply 
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down slightly from the 
€455bn forecast for 2026. In our view, several variables will be steering next year’s 
issuance. 
Redemptions will remain the central driver of the elevated 2027 bank bond supply 
forecast. Nearly €400bn of bonds mature in 2027, up €30bn year-on-year and the 
highest level in a decade. The increase is especially significant in the covered bond 
segment where €186bn will mature, a historic high. 
The subordinated segment will also rise, with redemptions totalling €45bn, up about 
€6bn from 2026. Of this total, €31bn is in Tier 2 instruments and €14bn in AT1s. The 
senior unsecured segment will partially offset these increases, with total redemptions 
reaching more than €162bn, down from more than €170bn recorded this year. The 
majority stems from senior bail-in bonds (senior non-preferred or senior HoldCo) with 
more than €90bn and an additional €71bn maturing in senior preferred instruments. 
Despite diverging trends across segments, elevated redemption volumes remain the 
main driver of bank issuance in the euro bond primary market, as institutions refinance 
maturing instruments.

Marine Leleux 
Sector Strategist, Financials 
marine.leleux2@ing.com

Jesse Norcross 
Senior Sector Strategist, Real Estate 
jesse.norcross@ing.com

Bank bond supply to stay at cruising 
altitude in 2027  
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027. Redemptions remain the 
main driver of the high supply, but the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected 
to push supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance reaching €185bn, 
senior unsecured bond supply at €210bn and another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds

### Page 4

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Bank bond redemptions per segment

Source: ING

While high redemption levels point to stronger supply in 2027, other factors also play a 
role, such as the macroeconomic environment. Eurozone GDP growth is forecast to 
increase slightly in the second half of next year to reach 1.5%, still well below levels seen 
after the pandemic. 
Furthermore, our economists pencil in an additional European Central Bank rate hike in 
2026, bringing policy rates to 2.75% in early 2027. The higher interest rate environment 
is not expected to support lending growth, especially as geopolitical instability remains. 
As a result, we do not see the modest macroeconomic backdrop acting as a catalyst for 
bank bond supply in 2027. 
Policy rates and GDP growth forecasts

Source: ING

Asset swap spread levels initially proved very resilient to geopolitical instability and 
related economic changes this year. Despite some volatility in March, following the 
outbreak of the US-Israel conflict with Iran, spread levels recovered quickly and reached 
levels close to their historic low. However, a broader escalation of the conflict across the 
Middle East, coupled with the absence of a clear path to resolution, could put further 
pressure on spreads. 
In addition, budget and political concerns in France and OAT-Bund volatility, as well as 
broader European fiscal concerns, have recently led to spreads widening again. The 
combination of French political uncertainty and the Middle East conflict could trigger 
some periods of volatility in the market in 2027. However, we believe these uncertainties 
will not be a strong negative for bank bond supply. Depending on the timing, though,

### Page 5

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

periodic widening may make financial institutions less keen to pre-fund redemptions 
coming up in 2028. 
Our 2027 supply forecast 
When accounting for these three main variables, we expect the EUR bank bond supply 
to reach €450bn in 2027. This translates into a net supply of €57bn, down from €89bn in 
2026. 
Forecast EUR bank bond supply for 2027

YTD = 16/10 
Source: ING

Our estimate includes €185bn in covered bonds, comprising €180bn in benchmark-sized 
issuances, including floating rate notes, and €5bn in sub-benchmark bonds. This is a 
€5bn drop versus our 2026 forecast. It reflects the pre-financing already made by issuers 
this year in preparation for the historically high redemptions. 
Furthermore, next year’s mortgage lending forecasts point to stagnation, somewhat 
limiting any further increase in covered bond issuance. Besides, with the securitisation 
package entering its finalisation stage, issuers with high funding needs may shift some 
secured issuance towards securitisation for diversification purposes. Increasing demand 
for covered bonds from overseas investors driven by regulatory developments may also 
result in more currency diversification, away from EUR-denominated issuance. 
We forecast senior unsecured supply to reach €210bn next year. This is split between 
€70bn in senior preferred bonds and €140bn in senior bail-in instruments. It represents a 
€10bn YoY decline in senior preferred supply, reflecting both the drop in the segment’s 
redemptions from 2027 onwards and the potential impact of the Crisis Management 
and Deposit Insurance (CMDI) framework. As the CMDI reforms are expected to enter 
into force in 2028, they could reduce the attractiveness of senior preferred bonds versus 
senior bail-in instruments in some EU countries, as senior preferred exposures will rank 
lower than, and no longer pari passu to, uncovered deposits in the creditor hierarchy. 
The funding cost advantage of senior preferred unsecured issuance could decline. At the 
same time, issuing senior non-preferred bonds could serve the purpose of increasing the 
bail-inable layer protecting preferred senior bonds and their current ratings. This could 
shift some issuers’ focus towards the senior non-preferred bucket instead of the senior 
preferred segment. 
Our forecast for senior non-preferred supply remains stable compared to our 2026 
estimate. While redemptions will increase by €6bn in 2027 and further in 2028 to reach 
€102bn, we believe the impact of higher refinancing needs on non-preferred senior 
supply will be partially offset by the lack of balance sheet growth against the backdrop 
of higher interest rates. Furthermore, the outperformance of US dollar debt versus EUR-

### Page 6

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

denominated debt could see issuers turn more often towards the USD market, 
potentially negatively affecting EUR senior bail-in supply. 
Lastly, we expect Tier 2 supply to reach €35bn and AT1 supply to reach €20bn in 2027. 
This is a €5bn increase YoY in both AT1 and Tier 2 compared to our 2026 forecast, mainly 
driven by higher redemption levels. With AT1 redemptions in 2027 increasing by €3bn 
YoY to €14bn, net supply is likely to be marginally higher next year. In Tier 2, 
redemptions increase by €4bn to €31bn, essentially meaning that net supply will only 
be marginally higher. 
As the chart below highlights, net supply in AT1 has been significant in recent years but 
has slowed this year as the focus has shifted towards refinancing. As such, while we 
expect more AT1 supply next year compared to 2026, the levels are relatively in line 
with the longer-term trend. 
Forecast EUR AT1 supply (€bn)

Source: ING

The main driver of supply will also be refinancing for subordinated capital. We expect a 
further marginal decline in CET1 buffers, although most banks are already operating at 
target AT1 and Tier 2 ratios. Still, there are some issuers with substantial gaps in either 
AT1 or Tier 2 ratios, which they may look to improve, leading to a slight increase in 
supply. 
Significant Risk Transfer (SRT) issuance has also increased in recent years and will 
continue to grow as a tool for banks in terms of balance sheet and capital management, 
while it is also growing as an investable asset class. Although SRTs provide capital and 
RWA relief, they do not replace the gone-concern and loss-absorption capacity of AT1 
and Tier 2 instruments. As such, they play a more complementary role in capital 
management, and we do not expect it to have a major impact on bank capital issuance 
in 2027. 
Bank M&A could also be a wildcard for 2027 issuance. With several large M&A 
transactions potentially taking place, this may lead to some capital optimisation and 
less incremental supply needs; conversely, goodwill effects, potential regulatory 
increases in buffer requirements or RWA inflation may lead to a slight increase in the 
need for capital. However, on the whole, we would expect M&A to have a slight 
dampening effect on supply. 
Finally, spreads are currently quite elevated, and coupons on new issues are generally 
higher than legacy bonds maturing or callable in 2027. This may also lead to a more 
wait-and-see approach from issuers, but we still think that most issuers will continue to 
drive their capital plans as in previous years.

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Sustainable bank bond supply is blooming, nearing a record in 2026

Banks issued an unexpectedly large amount of sustainable debt this year despite 
Europe’s ESG deregulation efforts. Up to mid-October 2026, they issued more than 
€86bn in sustainable bonds across different instrument types, up by almost €20bn from 
the same period last year. 
This was a surprisingly strong increase given that favourable primary market conditions 
offered issuers little greenium, despite continued evidence that sustainable bonds 
attract higher investor demand, as reflected in stronger oversubscription rates. This 
suggests that the availability of sustainable assets was not a meaningful constraint on 
banks' sustainable debt issuance, thanks to higher redemptions and sustainable lending 
growth. 
Issuers are also increasingly directing ESG bond issuance towards European capital 
markets, often at the expense of North American markets, reflecting the growing 
divergence in political and regulatory priorities between the two regions. 
The bulk of the YTD supply (€76.6bn) (re)financed green assets, with green supply up 
€25bn from 2025. Instead, issuance in social and sustainability formats did not exceed 
€9.6bn, trailing last year’s supply by €4.5bn. All instrument types, from secured to 
unsecured to subordinated, contributed to the higher ESG supply volumes. However, 
sustainable T2 and bail-in senior (senior non-preferred and HoldCo) issuance increased 
particularly strongly, as the funding cost advantage from sustainable issuance was 
better for these instruments than for covered bonds and preferred senior debt. 
Issuance under the European Green Bond Standard (EuGBS) rose from €2.5bn to almost 
€12bn YTD. For many banks, ICMA-aligned green bond issuance remained the more 
practical option as it allows them to finance a broader pool of assets, rather than fully 
commit the bond proceeds to Taxonomy-aligned activities. This is particularly relevant

What is driving growth in ESG supply in 2027? 
We expect sustainable bank bond supply to reach €100bn in FY2027, up from €93bn in 
FY2026 and equivalent to 22% of total euro-denominated bank bond supply. Green 
issuance remains dominant at €85bn, of which €17bn (20%) will be issued under the EU 
Green Bond Standard (EuGBS). Social, sustainable and sustainability-linked supply will 
total €15bn, of which 80% will be issued in social format. 
Greater availability of social assets, together with regulatory initiatives such as the 
Affordable Housing Act presented by the European Commission in September 2026, 
should help social issuance to gain further traction in 2027.

UniCredit and Commerzbank are at the centre of Europe’s closely watched push towards cross -border banking 
consolidation

European bank M&A is back 
European bank M&A has seen a strong resurgence, with several large-scale deals 
making headlines. UniCredit’s high-stakes takeover of Commerzbank, once deemed very 
unlikely but now moving closer to completion, could be Europe’s most significant cross-
border transaction in years. Italy remains one of Europe’s bank M&A hotbeds, with 
multiple banks taking part in the ongoing consolidation wave. Other banks have also 
been active, and we expect M&A to remain elevated in 2027. 
Year-to-date M&A deal value has already surpassed the 2025 total, reaching €53bn 
including the UniCredit-Commerzbank transaction and based on deals valued at €100m or 
more. Furthermore, deals pending or intended total €39bn, which includes the potential 
takeover of Banca Monte dei Paschi by Intesa Sanpaolo, currently valued at around €34bn. 
All in, this would bring deal value back to levels not seen since the early 2010s.

European bank M&A is back with a

European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and 2027 should stay busy. 
Domestic deals still dominate, while cross-border consolidation is harder but is also 
picking up. Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and execution still 
tt  E t  d l  b t  t f ti  f E '  b ki  t  i  till

### Page 14

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

The rationale for European bank M&A has changed. After the financial crisis, deals were 
mostly about repair, followed by a wave of deals involving stronger domestic banks 
buying weaker rivals. Today, the backdrop has shifted – profitability and capital levels 
are stronger, making the obvious cost-efficiency story less compelling on its own. 
Instead, banks are using M&A to add scale, deposits and fee-generating diversification, 
while the need to close the gap with US rivals remains a key driver. 
The industrial logic has also strengthened, as fixed costs are rising in areas such as 
technology, AI, compliance, as well as personnel. Rising investment requirements mean 
that remaining subscale is becoming more expensive, even for highly profitable banks. 
Domestic dominates, but cross-border competes 
UniCredit-Commerzbank remains the sector’s most closely watched deal and could 
become the first major cross-border acquisition since the financial crisis. UniCredit has 
slowly been building its stake rather than a clean takeover and is now deemed to hold 
effective control, despite currently owning around 50% of the shares. Furthermore, it is 
also not entirely a leap into an unfamiliar market, as UniCredit already has a significant 
banking business in Germany through its subsidiary, HVB. 
The deal has also faced considerable pushback as well as political and stakeholder 
opposition, but is now looking increasingly likely to proceed. It has highlighted the 
difficulty of pulling off a large-scale cross-border deal for European banks. Yet it is now 
becoming the clearest live test of whether Europe is genuinely ready for cross-border 
bank consolidation. 
Domestic deals remain the rule, but we have seen other examples of cross-border deals. 
These include BPCE’s acquisition of Portuguese peer Novo Banco; Erste Bank's acquisition 
of Santander Bank Polska; and Crédit Agricole acquiring IKB to increase its presence in 
Germany. Some banks are also looking across the pond, such as Santander, which 
acquired Webster Financial Corporation in the US for USD12.5bn. 
These transactions are important, but they do not yet amount to a broad-based cross-
border consolidation wave. Many involve banks already familiar with the target market 
or assets that complement an existing local business.

### Page 15

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Italy is still riding the wave 
Italy has become one of Europe’s busiest M&A markets, riding a new wave of 
consolidation, with Banca Monte dei Paschi (MPS) at the epicentre. The bank completed 
the acquisition of Mediobanca in 2025, and this year approached Banco BPM and Banca 
Generali for a merger as a counteroffensive to Intesa Sanpaolo’s hostile bid for MPS. 
The counteroffensive now appears less likely, as key shareholders have opposed parts of 
MPS’s merger plans and have also backed Intesa’s bid. The ECB has also provided 
authorisation to take control of MPS, representing an important step for the €34bn 
takeover. Alongside this, we had the BPER–Popolare di Sondrio merger earlier in the year, 
adding to Italy’s consolidation. 
Why is Italy so active in particular? Well, Italy remains quite fragmented (see below) and 
still offers a large domestic synergy pool, even after the wave of M&A that has already 
taken place. Furthermore, the country has meaningful industrial scale and offers further 
revenue diversification opportunities for banks and insurers. Although the obvious 
combinations are becoming less evident in Italy, we expect this to continue to drive M&A 
activity in 2027. 
Top 5 banks' lending market share by country (2025)

Source: AFME, ING

It's not only about getting bigger 
The deal mix is also broadening, as banks are increasingly focusing on fee income as 
well as scale, while also stepping into capital-light businesses. Deals in sectors such as 
wealth management, asset management, payments & FX and insurance are likely to 
feature prominently. 
NatWest Group’s acquisition of Evelyn Partners is a good example. The combination 
created the UK’s largest Private Banking and Wealth Management business, while lifting 
Natwest’s fee income by 20%. Similarly, BNP Paribas’s acquisition of AXA Investment 
Managers in 2025 created a €1.5tn asset management business, as the bank is seeking 
to compete with US asset management rivals and grow its fee income. In Germany, 
Deutsche Bank and DWS were reported to be exploring a potential minority investment 
in the life insurance business Frankfurter Leben. This would mark Deutsche’s return to 
life insurance after more than a decade. 
Consolidation can support bank performance 
But does consolidation actually deliver better outcomes? The evidence is not entirely 
clear-cut, but overall points in the right direction. While increasing scale is not always 
the best use of capital, in general, more consolidated markets tend to have more stable 
and, in some cases, higher profitability. The ECB is in favour of further consolidation 
where appropriate, as it sees the benefits of diversification and efficiency. On the other 
hand, capital can be reduced following a merger. This could be a downside, as better 
20%

51% 53% 61% 64%

100%

### Page 16

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

capitalised banks tend to enjoy lower funding costs and thus have more room to lend to 
the real economy. 
The outcome therefore depends on the trade-off between longer-term synergies and 
the upfront capital, execution and integration costs. 
ECB data show an association between more concentrated banking markets and 
profitability. As the chart below shows, countries with a high level of concentration 
(defined by a higher Herfindahl-Hirschman Index coefficient) tend to have higher net 
interest margins on average. Still, net interest margins in Europe are structurally lower 
than in the US, where they tend to be between 3–4%. 
Net interest margin vs market concentration for European countries (2025)

Source: ECB, ING

Similarly, a higher level of market concentration in European countries is associated with 
a lower cost of risk. The correlation is modest, with some clear outliers, but the numbers 
show that greater concentration can lead to lower asset quality costs, although the 
relationship should perhaps not be interpreted as causal. Still, taken together, this 
suggests that consolidation can improve banking markets. 
Cost of risk vs market concentration for European countries (2025)

Source: ECB, ING

Regulation is helping, but can get in the way 
The Banking Union has made deals easier to assess: the ECB has become the single 
prudential supervisor for large banks, capital rules are more consistent, while resolution 
planning is now much clearer than it was even a decade ago. But as the Draghi report 
noted, the incomplete Banking Union means Europe’s banks remain less profitable than 
their US counterparts.

### Page 17

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

There is a shift underway, though. The EU Commission plans to table a broad Banking 
Package in the first quarter of 2027, with measures covering cross-border capital and 
liquidity management, deposit insurance, prudential rules and other items. For M&A, the 
most important proposals include those to give banking groups more flexibility to 
allocate capital and liquidity more efficiently within cross-border groups, while also 
applying more consistent treatment to intra-group exposures. These remain proposals 
rather than agreed implementations, meaning their final scope and timetable remain 
uncertain. 
The AFME estimates that regulatory ring-fencing rules mean that some €225bn of 
capital and €250bn of liquidity is trapped within subsidiaries, which banks cannot freely 
move between parent and subsidiaries. Freeing this up could also support more cross-
border transactions. 
Regulatory ring-fencing traps subsidiaries' capital and liquidity

Source: ECB, ING

The European Commission is also bringing forward a new proposal to replace the 
European Deposit Insurance Scheme (EDIS), which could reduce national ring-fencing 
incentives and encourage more cross-border M&A. CMDI reform has been passed this 
year, which could make it easier to deal with failing banks, potentially making 
consolidation easier as well, although the direct impact on M&A is unclear. 
The Danish compromise, which is now embedded into CRR3, allows banks to risk-weight 
certain insurance subsidiaries rather than deduct them from regulatory capital. This was 
clarified by the EBA in January 2026, and some banks such as UniCredit have received 
authorisation to use the Danish Compromise methodology for its insurance subsidiaries. 
This has improved the economics of bancassurance and could support further bank-
insurance transactions. 
On the other hand, national regulatory and political intervention has also put the brakes 
on M&A in some cases, raising execution costs and uncertainty. BBVA’s proposed 
acquisition of Sabadell in 2024 illustrates the problem. It took 539 days from the 
announcement in May 2024 to the offer failing in October 2025. 
For us, the message seems clear that European regulators are seeking to improve bank 
competitiveness, and they see consolidation as an important part of this. Looking 
ahead, regulation can improve the plumbing, but it is not a dealmaker. 
Why 2027 should stay busy 
The starting point for 2027 remains supportive, with bank profitability continuing to hold 
up well in 2026 while capital levels remain solid. While the geopolitical, inflation and 
interest rate backdrop poses headwinds, we still expect acquisition activity to remain 
high. On the other hand, management teams will increasingly need to choose between 
€225bn
€250bn
Capital Liquidity

### Page 18

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

retaining greater capital headroom in a less market-friendly environment, shareholder 
payouts, or M&A. 
The most likely deals are still domestic. Italy remains the M&A market of choice, but 
other geographies could also stand out. The Nordics are likely to see more partnerships 
and tech- and wealth-focused deals. Spain and Portugal are already quite concentrated, 
so we could see more bolt-ons. In the UK, challenger banks and neobanks are gaining 
ground while deposit competition remains intense, potentially leading to more activity. 
The Benelux has potential catalysts following a series of government ownership 
reductions and increasing inorganic ambitions. 
Cross-border activity could also remain elevated, but we think the real litmus test 
remains the UniCredit-Commerzbank deal, including what happens after completion in 
terms of execution and integration, a process that is likely to take a few years. The 
regulatory environment should also become increasingly more supportive for cross-
border M&A from 2027 onwards. 
Overall, 2027 could be another interesting year. 
M&A is here to stay 
European bank M&A has picked up in recent years, and we expect more activity again in 
2027, although we do not see a big bang. For investors, positioning is inherently 
speculative. For bondholders, the main way to gain is by owning the target entity's 
bonds. However, this is not always the case, and bigger doesn’t always mean better, as 
credit ratings don’t necessarily improve either following an acquisition or merger. 
We are not suggesting that a major change in the European banking landscape is 
imminent, as many obstacles remain, particularly with cross-border deals. But the 
direction of travel is interesting: larger banks often outperform smaller banks, while 
regulators continue to see benefits from a deeper, more integrated European banking 
union. Still, European banking markets remain fragmented to differing degrees, both 
between countries and compared to the US, presenting both opportunities and 
obstacles.

### Page 19

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Bank capital enters 2027 on solid ground, while tight spreads and rising risks favour selectivity

AT1 reform remains on the back burner 
The European Commission published its report on competitiveness in the banking sector 
in July but did not include much in the way of proposals for AT1 bank capital 
instruments. The report mainly focused on reducing complexity, reporting requirements 
and improving competitiveness for Europe’s banking sector. 
For AT1, this probably means that the instruments are unlikely to be phased out 
anytime soon. What is somewhat relevant for AT1s is the Commission’s intention to 
reduce complexity for bank capital structures. This includes making the Pillar 2 
requirement and guidance more transparent, while the Commission also proposes to 
consolidate parts of the buffers into two buffers: one that can be released in periods of 
stress, and another that would be maintained in all scenarios. 
This, in theory, could reduce the risk of coupon skips, as being able to release buffers 
during periods of stress would not automatically lead to a breach of the combined buffer 
requirements. The proposals also include a removal of the leverage ratio Pillar 2 capital 
requirement, which would affect the largest European systemically important banks 
subject to this requirement. This is only a small number of banks but could (in theory) 
lead to a marginal decline in AT1 issuance. 
Overall, AT1 reform remains part of a broader discussion, with the EU Commission 
proposals also likely to change as negotiations remain ongoing and consensus needs to 
be built. For AT1, we expect the instruments to keep playing an important role (yay!) in 
European banks’ capital stacks, with no signs of an imminent abolition or grandfathering 
process. 
More supply while refinancing remains the main event 
With no major changes to AT1 structures or requirements imminent, we don’t expect a 
significant impact on banks’ capital instrument management and funding plans in 2027.
